Oh, worrying? Well, I suppose everyone worries a little bit, don't they? Especially these days, there's always something on the news to fret over. But I wouldn't say I worry a *lot*, no.

I just wouldn't want you to think I'm some sort of nervous wreck!

I promise I'm usually much sharper than this. I just followed the signs for psychiatry when I came in. Is... is that enough of an answer, or did you need the exact address? I could try to look in my purse for the appointment card if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to manage your bills and medications on your own?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my husband is such a dear, he really is. I've always taken pride in managing the house and our affairs, but... well, I suppose I have been letting him help out a little bit more lately. Just to be safe, you know! \n\nI felt so terribly silly about it, but I actually missed a few of my doctor visits recently, and... oh, I got the senior center schedule just completely tangled up. It was a bit of a mess, I'm afraid, and I felt awful for the confusion. So, my husband has stepped in to help with the phone calls and keeping the calendars straight. He’s even taken over making decisions about home repairs that I always used to handle myself. \n\nI hope it doesn't make me sound completely incapable, letting him do all that... I just didn't want to cause any more trouble with my little mix-ups. I suppose he helps keep my medications on track now with the calendar, too. Is it alright that he's helping me with those things?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, depressed? No, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I mean, we all have our little blue days, don't we? Especially at my age, you know, when you can't quite do everything you used to. But I try to stay positive! \n\nI really wouldn't want to complain or make a fuss. I have so much to be thankful for, especially with my husband being so wonderful and patient with me. I just try to keep a smile on my face and not let things get me down too much. I hope that's the right way to look at it? I certainly wouldn't want you to worry about me being miserable or anything like that!\n\nDoctor: Did these memory problems come on gradually over time, or did they start suddenly?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose it's been more of a gradual thing, really. It wasn't like I woke up one morning and suddenly couldn't remember a thing! Oh, heavens no. It's just been little things here and there over the last few years, creeping up on me, I suppose. \n\nAt first, I thought it was just normal, you know? Just getting a bit older and having a few \"senior moments.\" Everyone has those, right? I'd misplace my keys or forget why I walked into a room, but I always figured it was nothing to make a fuss over. It's only recently that it's gotten a bit more... noticeable, I guess. But it definitely wasn't all at once. I hope that makes sense? I wouldn't want you to think my mind just went blank overnight!\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cold lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, cold? Goodness, no, I wouldn't say unusually so. I mean, well, you know how it is when you get to be my age, sometimes the draft in the house feels a little brisker than it used to. But nothing out of the ordinary! \n\nMy husband always makes sure I have a nice warm sweater if I need one, bless him. I certainly haven't been shivering or anything like that. Just the normal chill now and then, which is perfectly fine, really. I hope that's alright? I wouldn't want you to think I'm freezing all the time or complaining about the temperature!\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any numbness or tingling in your hands or feet?\n\nPatient: Oh, numbness or tingling? No, I really haven't noticed anything like that. I mean, sometimes if I sit in one position for too long, my foot might fall asleep for a moment, but that happens to everyone, doesn't it? \n\nI certainly haven't had any strange feelings in my hands or feet that would be bothersome. I try to stay as active as I can, keeping moving and all that. I haven't had any trouble with my hands when I'm doing my little chores around the house. So, no, nothing to worry about there, I'm quite sure. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information!\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things?\n\nPatient: Oh, worrying? Well, I suppose everyone worries a little bit, don't they? Especially these days, there's always something on the news to fret over. But I wouldn't say I worry a *lot*, no. \n\nI mean, I like to make sure things are taken care of—that the bills are paid and the house is tidy, just normal things. I wouldn't want to be a bother to anyone by letting things slip. But I certainly don't sit around wringing my hands all day! I try to keep a positive outlook, I really do. I hope that's the kind of answer you're looking for? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm some sort of nervous wreck!\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble with your balance when walking?\n\nPatient: Oh, my balance? No, thank goodness, I've been very lucky with that. I mean... well, I suppose I take my time a little more than I used to, and I always make sure to hold onto the banister when I go down the stairs. But that's just being sensible at my age, isn't it? \n\nI certainly haven't been stumbling around or falling over, oh goodness no. I always wear good, sturdy shoes around the house so I don't have an accident. I try very hard to be careful so I don't end up causing a fuss or being a burden to my husband. Does that answer what you were looking for? I just wouldn't want you to worry that I'm getting frail or anything like that!\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble finding the right words when you’re speaking?\n\nPatient: Oh, finding the right words? Well, no, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. Like, oh, appointment times, or the dates for the little exhibits we do, or I'll completely forget which volunteer I've already called and end up ringing the same poor dear twice. \n\nIt's a bit embarrassing to admit, if I'm being honest. I know at my age a little slip of the mind happens, so I hope I'm not wasting your time by bringing it up. Is that... is that the sort of thing you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: What is today’s date?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... today's date. Well, isn't that just exactly the sort of thing I was talking about? Let me think... um... well, to be perfectly honest with you, Doctor, the exact number has just completely slipped my mind right this second. \n\nI usually check my kitchen calendar right when I make my morning tea, but I was just so focused on getting here on time so I wouldn't keep you waiting! I'm so sorry, I must sound terribly disorganized. I hope it's alright that I don't have the exact day for you?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me where we are right now?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes, of course! Well, we're at your clinic, aren't we? The... oh, dear. I know it's a medical building, and I know exactly how to get here from the bus stop, but the specific name of the clinic or the hospital... it's just on the tip of my tongue. \n\nI'm so sorry, I feel like I'm failing a test! I promise I'm usually much sharper than this. I just followed the signs for psychiatry when I came in. Is... is that enough of an answer, or did you need the exact address? I could try to look in my purse for the appointment card if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to manage your bills and medications on your own?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my husband is such a dear, he really is. I've always taken pride in managing the house and our affairs, but... well, I suppose I have been letting him help out a little bit more lately. Just to be safe, you know! \n\nI felt so terribly silly about it, but I actually missed a few of my doctor visits recently, and... oh, I got the senior center schedule just completely tangled up. It was a bit of a mess, I'm afraid, and I felt awful for the confusion. So, my husband has stepped in to help with the phone calls and keeping the calendars straight. He’s even taken over making decisions about home repairs that I always used to handle myself. \n\nI hope it doesn't make me sound completely incapable, letting him do all that... I just didn't want to cause any more trouble with my little mix-ups. I suppose he helps keep my medications on track now with the calendar, too. Is it alright that he's helping me with those things?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, depressed? No, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I mean, we all have our little blue days, don't we? Especially at my age, you know, when you can't quite do everything you used to. But I try to stay positive! \n\nI really wouldn't want to complain or make a fuss. I have so much to be thankful for, especially with my husband being so wonderful and patient with me. I just try to keep a smile on my face and not let things get me down too much. I hope that's the right way to look at it? I certainly wouldn't want you to worry about me being miserable or anything like that!\n\nDoctor: Did these memory problems come on gradually over time, or did they start suddenly?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose it's been more of a gradual thing, really. It wasn't like I woke up one morning and suddenly couldn't remember a thing! Oh, heavens no. It's just been little things here and there over the last few years, creeping up on me, I suppose. \n\nAt first, I thought it was just normal, you know? Just getting a bit older and having a few \"senior moments.\" Everyone has those, right? I'd misplace my keys or forget why I walked into a room, but I always figured it was nothing to make a fuss over. It's only recently that it's gotten a bit more... noticeable, I guess. But it definitely wasn't all at once. I hope that makes sense? I wouldn't want you to think my mind just went blank overnight!\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cold lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, cold? Goodness, no, I wouldn't say unusually so. I mean, well, you know how it is when you get to be my age, sometimes the draft in the house feels a little brisker than it used to. But nothing out of the ordinary! \n\nMy husband always makes sure I have a nice warm sweater if I need one, bless him. I certainly haven't been shivering or anything like that. Just the normal chill now and then, which is perfectly fine, really. I hope that's alright? I wouldn't want you to think I'm freezing all the time or complaining about the temperature!\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any numbness or tingling in your hands or feet?\n\nPatient: Oh, numbness or tingling? No, I really haven't noticed anything like that. I mean, sometimes if I sit in one position for too long, my foot might fall asleep for a moment, but that happens to everyone, doesn't it? \n\nI certainly haven't had any strange feelings in my hands or feet that would be bothersome. I try to stay as active as I can, keeping moving and all that. I haven't had any trouble with my hands when I'm doing my little chores around the house. So, no, nothing to worry about there, I'm quite sure. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information!\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things?\n\nPatient: Oh, worrying? Well, I suppose everyone worries a little bit, don't they? Especially these days, there's always something on the news to fret over. But I wouldn't say I worry a *lot*, no. \n\nI mean, I like to make sure things are taken care of—that the bills are paid and the house is tidy, just normal things. I wouldn't want to be a bother to anyone by letting things slip. But I certainly don't sit around wringing my hands all day! I try to keep a positive outlook, I really do. I hope that's the kind of answer you're looking for? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm some sort of nervous wreck!\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble with your balance when walking?\n\nPatient: Oh, my balance? No, thank goodness, I've been very lucky with that. I mean... well, I suppose I take my time a little more than I used to, and I always make sure to hold onto the banister when I go down the stairs. But that's just being sensible at my age, isn't it? \n\nI certainly haven't been stumbling around or falling over, oh goodness no. I always wear good, sturdy shoes around the house so I don't have an accident. I try very hard to be careful so I don't end up causing a fuss or being a burden to my husband. Does that answer what you were looking for? I just wouldn't want you to worry that I'm getting frail or anything like that!\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble finding the right words when you’re speaking?\n\nPatient: Oh, finding the right words? Well, no, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. I mean... sometimes I might pause for just a second to remember an actor's name or something silly like that, but that's just a normal part of getting a little older, isn't it? \n\nI certainly haven't been forgetting how to speak or anything dreadful like that. I still do my daily crossword puzzles to keep my mind sharp, you know. I try very hard to make sure I'm staying on top of things. I hope I'm making sense right now? I just wouldn't want you to think my mind is slipping away from me, because it really isn't! Is that what you were checking for?\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to do your grocery shopping on your own?\n\nPatient: Oh, the grocery shopping? Yes, of course, I still manage that just fine. I mean, I like to pick out my own produce, you know? But... well, I suppose my husband has been helping out a little bit more with some of the other things lately. Just the boring paperwork sort of things! \n\nI did get a little bit mixed up with the calendar a while back—nothing serious, just missed a doctor's appointment and got the days tangled up for my activities at the senior center. It's so easy to do when you're busy, isn't it? So my husband has just been giving me a hand with making the phone calls and keeping the schedule straight. And he's been handling the decisions when things need fixing around the house, which is nice of him. I used to do all of that myself, but it's nice to share the load, don't you think? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm not capable, because I really am! I just let him help out so he feels useful, you know? Is that the sort of thing you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: Do you need help managing your finances?\n\nPatient: Oh, the finances? No, no, I don't need any help with that. I mean... well, my husband has always been so good with numbers, so he usually looks over the bank statements when they come in. But that's just how we've always done things, really. \n\nI certainly haven't been forgetting to pay the bills or doing anything silly with our money. I'm always very careful to make sure everything is in order. I still balance my own little checkbook for the things I buy, just to keep track, you know? I hope that's alright? I just wouldn't want you to think I can't handle my own affairs, because I promise I can. I just let him do the heavy lifting with the big accounts because he enjoys it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any medical condition that might affect your memory, such as thyroid problems, vitamin B12 deficiency, or a neurological illness?\n\nPatient: Oh, a medical condition? No, no, nothing like that. My doctor says I'm actually quite healthy for my age! I mean... I do take a little pill for my blood pressure, but that's just standard, isn't it? \n\nI certainly haven't been told I have any sort of thyroid issue or... or anything wrong with my brain. Goodness, no! I take a nice multivitamin every morning, just to be on the safe side, so my vitamins should be perfectly fine. I try very hard to take good care of myself, you know? I go to all my check-ups and do exactly what the doctor tells me. I hope that's the right answer? I just wouldn't want you to worry that there's some big, scary illness hiding in the background, because there really isn't. I'm doing quite well, honestly! Is that what you were looking for?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
